218 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 348. F00COW DISTRICT. RIVER MIN. ROCKY POINT BEACON. NoTicE is hereby given that a beacon, named the Rock&#39; Point Beacon, has been erected on the ledge of rocks existing about half-way between Tunui and Tintao, and bearing from Half-tide Beacon W. by S. j S., distant 8 cables. The beacon is situated about 8o feet from the outer extremity of the ledge of rocks. It consists of a spar surmounted by a cage, which is elevated about 13 feet above high water. The beacon is painted red, and on the cage the words &quot; ROCKY POINT &quot; are painted. Vessels are recommended not to approach within I cable of the beacon. SHANGHAI, 25th April ioo. No. 349. SWATOW 0 ISTRICT. NOTICE is hereby given that Captain D. MACKENZIE, of the s.s. Kweilin, reports that his ship struck bottom at 4.5 A.M. on the 4th instant, with East Point, Namoa Island, bearing north true, and Three Chimney Bluff Point bearing S. 70 W. true, the Kweili4 drawing at the time, at the point of contact (the bilge keel), about 14 feet. In the above position British Admiralty Chart No. 1,957, small corrections to January 1893, shows 14 fathoms of water. SHANGHAI, I Ith June 1oo.
1900.] NOTIFICATIONS. 21 No. 350. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. CHANGE IN CHARACTER OF GUTZLAFF LIGHT. NoTICE is hereby given that the character of Gutz1aff Light has been changed. The new illuminating apparatus is dioptric, lightning-flashing, of the third order, showing one white flash every 5 seconds. The new lighthouse stands on the eastern end of the summit of the island, 144 feet N. 77V. K, magnetic, from the site of the old light; and the light, which is elevated 283 feet above the level of the sea, should be visible in clear weather at a distance of 23.7 nautical miles. The tower is a steel structure, 25 feet high, with a total height from the base to the lantern vane of 461 feet. The tower is painted white. APPENDIX I. KOWLOON A 4D LAPPA: QUARTERLYw RETURNS OF TRADE. APRIL-JUNE 1900.
CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-,UN]Es (Received i8th July 19oo.) CUSTOM OUS, KOWLOON, 13TB JULY 190o. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmi -to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbourin stations in Native vessels for the second quarter of the year 19oo, April-June. b. The Custom House (HE H) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium &#39;Hk.Ta 30 and Hk.2I 8o pe&#39;r picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the raCes fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the Ist July 1887. T&#39;he stations then controlled by the Kowloon Customs were known as Capsuimoon (M * P9),&#39;Changchow (R N), Fotochow (f Rfi ), and Kowloon (*, K). On the 5th October 1899, as a result -of the extension of territory to form part of the Colony of Hongkong, they were removed to Taishan (* ), Lintin (a ff), and Shamchun (M jJ), on the north and west; to Samaun (= fE), on the east; and to Shaiichung(g ), on the Mirs Bay littoral. The Custom House is now called Ag f U. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II. 238 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, (Received 18th July i9o.) CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, 12TH JULY 1900. I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 38T, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the second quarter of the year i9oo, April-June. b. The Custom House (W * ) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.T&amp;amp; 3o and Hk.a 8o per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com- menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the Ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow (. ), Tungho (A $), and Chienshan (Bl LJj) stations, with three sub-dtations, known as the Shekkok ( ), Kuttai ( *), and Kwanchiiap (I fg) sub-stations. c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
